Use first, before drafting, when discipline fit is uncertain. Use when results stop at significance or at a vivid story without an explicit mechanism. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-mechanism-social-process) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-problematic**: Use to build a sociological problem consciousness (问题意识) for 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — turning a variable relationship or a pile of fieldwork into a question about how a social mechanism, structure, or process works. Use when the manuscript still reads as "X affects Y" without a sociological stake. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-problematic) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-qualitative**: Use for the qualitative path in 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — fieldwork ethnography, in-depth interviews, the extended case method, or grounded theory. - **socs-quantitative**: Use for the quantitative path in 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — analyzing survey data (CGSS/CFPS/CLDS) with regression / Logit / multilevel / event-history (survival) / sequence analysis, and interpreting results as a sociological mechanism rather than entering an econometric identification arms race. - **socs-rebuttal**: Use to draft the response letter for an R&R at 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — addressing reviewer concerns about sociological problematic, theory dialogue, mechanism/social process, qualitative trustworthiness, or quantitative interpretation, point by point, with respect and evidence. - **socs-submission**: Use for the pre-submission preflight to 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — checking in-text author-date citation (作者, 年: 页) plus end references (GB/T 7714, A–Z, 中外文分列), explanatory footnotes, abstract/keyword limits, the official online system (the journal's only channel), anonymity, and document hygiene. - **soctheory-argument-development**: Use when stress-testing the reasoning of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript — turning stated propositions into a valid, warranted argument that survives rival theories and counter-cases. Develops and audits the argument; it does NOT define the concepts (soctheory-theory-construction) or bound the theory's domain (soctheory-boundary-conditions). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-argument-development) · ★1.1k)
- **soctheory-boundary-conditions**: Use when specifying the scope, domain, and limits of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript's theory — stating where it holds, where it does not, and what it does NOT claim. Bounds the theory as a contribution; it does NOT build the concepts (soctheory-theory-construction) or audit the reasoning (soctheory-argument-development). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-boundary-conditions) · ★1.1k)
- **soctheory-conceptual-exhibits**: Use when building the figures and typologies of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript — mechanism diagrams, process models, 2x2 typologies, and concept maps that carry theoretical work (never regression tables or data plots). Designs conceptual exhibits; it does NOT build the underlying theory (soctheory-theory-construction) or write the prose (soctheory-writing-style). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-conceptual-exhibits) · ★1.1k)
- **soctheory-contribution-framing**: Use when articulating the theoretical contribution of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript — naming the new way of seeing it provides and differentiating it from prior theory. Frames the contribution; it does NOT build the theory (soctheory-theory-construction) or audit its reasoning (soctheory-argument-development). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-contribution-framing) · ★1.1k)
- **soctheory-literature-positioning**: Use when locating a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript in a tradition and conversation — naming whose theory you extend, challenge, or synthesize, and avoiding sibling-journal misattribution. - **soctheory-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response document for a Sociological Theory (ST) Revise & Resubmit — structuring point-by-point replies that show the theory was genuinely strengthened, not just defended. - **soctheory-review-process**: Use when you need to understand Sociological Theory (ST)'s double-anonymous peer review and interpret a decision letter focused on conceptual novelty and logical soundness. - **soctheory-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript — Manuscript Central portal, ASA format and length cap, abstract, masking for double-anonymous review, ASA-style references, fee, ethics/originality, and required files. - **soctheory-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether an idea is a genuine theoretical problem and a fit for Sociological Theory (ST) rather than an empirical study or a review essay. ([ComeOnOliver/skillshub](https://github.com/ComeOnOliver/skillshub/tree/HEAD/skills/TerminalSkills/skills/soda) · ★63 · 2 copies)
- **soda-artifact-evaluation**: Use when deciding how code, computations, or data attach to a SODA (ACM-SIAM Symposium on Discrete Algorithms) paper — SODA itself runs no artifact track, so this skill covers computer-assisted proof evidence, implementation companions, and when to route the artifact to co-located ALENEX's formal artifact evaluation instead. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/SODA-Skills/skills/soda-artifact-evaluation) · ★1.1k)
- **soda-author-response**: Use when drafting the SODA (ACM-SIAM Symposium on Discrete Algorithms) rebuttal — the roughly three-day window between review release in early September and the response deadline, triaging referee misreadings versus real gaps, and writing terse theorem-anchored replies inside HotCRP without breaking lightweight double-blind rules. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/SODA-Skills/skills/soda-author-response) · ★1.1k)
- **soda-basics**: Writes and runs data quality checks with Soda Core and the Soda Checks Language: freshness, volume, schema, and custom SQL checks. Use when adding lightweight, YAML-first quality checks to pipelines. Don't use for SQL-embedded transformation tests (use dbt tests) or for heavyweight expectation suites (use great-expectations). ([wiljav/DESkills](https://github.com/wiljav/DESkills/tree/HEAD/skills/quality/soda-basics))

- **soda-camera-ready**: Use when converting a SODA (ACM-SIAM Symposium on Discrete Algorithms) acceptance into a correct SIAM proceedings entry — following the October final-version instructions, condensing the no-limit submission into the proceedings version, restoring author identity, syncing the arXiv full version, and planning the January talk. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/SODA-Skills/skills/soda-camera-ready) · ★1.1k)
- **soda-checks**: Authors and runs SodaCL (Soda Checks Language) checks against SQL warehouses (Snowflake, BigQuery, Postgres, Redshift, etc.) via `soda scan`, configures scan profiles in configuration.yml, and gates CI on scan exit code. Use when the user works with Soda Core / Soda Cloud or needs YAML-driven warehouse data quality.
